## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a clear route: scale and position on slide 2, proof of results on slide 3, resilience on slide 4, then a strategy map on slide 5 that returns as numbered badges on slides 6 to 10 and pays off with the returns trajectory on slide 11. Slides 3, 4 and 7 are dense but well organised, with action titles, consistent navy and grey-blue bars that mark the latest year, bracketed change arrows, and a one-line takeaway at the foot of each page. The weaknesses are small type on the dense chart panels (slides 3 and 4 read as reference pages), unlabelled bars on slide 7 and the market share bars, and a plain text disclaimer and three pages of tiny footnotes at the end. It is disciplined and consistent but not a reference example.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the numbered strategy thread from slide 5 through slides 6 to 10, the navy highlight for the current year, the change arrows with CAGR callouts, and the closing takeaway line on each page.

**Most useful repair:** Raise the smallest type on slides 3, 4 and 7 and add values to the unlabelled bars on slide 7 and slide 11's driver mini-charts so the outcomes can be read without a spoken explanation.

**What would change the score:** Split slide 3 or trim its six chart panels so fewer numbers compete, and give slide 2's tree diagram and slide 9's table a stronger focal point.

_Confidence high. 15 of 15 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide for Shahmir Khaliq, Head of Services, and a Services overview covering Investor Services, Treasury and Trade Solutions and Liquidity Management, with headline network and scale figures. It then shows performance against targets through market share and financial charts, followed by a page on how Services stays resilient through rate cycles, supported by client relationships and fee drivers. Next come industry trends and the strategy's key focus areas, a slide on growth by client segment and vertical, and sections on next-generation platforms, AI initiatives, innovative solutions and digital asset strategy. A slide sets out near-term and through-the-cycle return targets, a forward-looking statements disclaimer follows, and footnotes for each slide close the deck. Slides worth studying include slide 3, which pairs multi-year bar charts with market share callouts, slide 4, which links rate charts to fee and deposit drivers, slide 7, which uses large metric tiles for platform outcomes, and slide 11, which lays out returns trajectory against key drivers.

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the Citi '26 Investor Day deck have?**

The Citi deck has 15 slides, and its footnotes begin on Slide 13. The footnotes for each slide close the deck.

**What financial performance does Citi report for 2025 in its investor day deck?**

Slide 3 reports 2025 revenues of $22.6B, NIR of $7.6B, EBT of $11.4B, and RoTCE of 24.6%. The same slide also lists average deposits of $878B and an efficiency ratio of 48%.

**What market share does Citi report for its Services business in its deck?**

Slide 2 shows Services at #1 RANK with an 11.5% SHARE, citing Coalition Greenwich Global Competitor Benchmarking Analytics for FY25 in footnote 1. The slide describes this as Citi being the unmatched leader for institutions with cross-border needs.

**What does Citi's deck say about its AI initiatives?**

Slide 8 gives a target developer productivity gain of ~30-40% from developers using agentic AI tools. It also reports an 80% reduction in U.S. account opening deal document processing time from Intelligent Document Processing.

Unmatched leader for institutions with cross-border needs / Services / #1 RANK 11.5% SHARE(1) / PAYMENTS / Make cross-border and / domestic payments and / collect from customers / LIQUIDITY / MANAGEMENT / SERVICES / Optimize liquidity / management globally / TRADE AND / WORKING CAPITAL / SOLUTIONS / Finance trade activities / and manage working / capital needs / INVESTOR / SERVICES / Connect investors and / intermediaries to markets / around the world / ISSUER / SERVICES / Support the capital market / issuance and transaction / execution needs of / global institutions / TREASURY AND TRADE SOLUTIONS / SECURITIES SERVICES / Citi is on / the ground in / 90+ countries(a) / ~17K clients(2), / including ~80% of / Fortune 500(3) / ~$6T(4) payments / processed daily / TO 180+ / COUNTRIES(a, b) / ~$1T / Average / deposits(5) / $31T AUC/A(b) / 100+ COUNTRY / CUSTODY / NETWORK(a, b, 6) / ~75% / of revenues from / Services + Markets / + Banking clients(7) / Services is the heart of Citi’s global network / Footnotes begin on Slide 13 / 2

**Layout**

Full-width white slide with the title left-aligned across the top at about 5% of the slide height, starting at x 2.8%. Below it a three-tier hierarchy is centred: one dark Services box at top centre, five rounded light-grey cards in a row (the first three with the connector bus, the grouping brackets beneath), then six pale blue-grey stat boxes in a row, and a centred tagline with a rule on each side near the bottom. Footnote text sits bottom-left and the page number bottom-right, at about 97.5% of the height.

**Visuals**

Organisational diagram, no photographs or logos. A dark navy rounded rectangle labelled Services with the line '#1 RANK' and '11.5% SHARE(1)' sits at top centre (x 38.8%, y 13.0%, 22.5% by 11.3%). A navy elbow connector runs from its bottom edge at x 50% down to a horizontal bus at about y 27.6% spanning from about x 11.4% to x 88.6%, with vertical drops of about 3 to 4 percent of slide height into the top of each of the five cards. Five light-grey cards labelled PAYMENTS, LIQUIDITY MANAGEMENT SERVICES, TRADE AND WORKING CAPITAL SOLUTIONS, INVESTOR SERVICES and ISSUER SERVICES each carry a short navy rule beneath the heading. Beneath the first three cards a navy bracket spans them, labelled TREASURY AND TRADE SOLUTIONS; beneath the last two a second bracket is labelled SECURITIES SERVICES. Six pale blue-grey boxes below hold statistics: Citi is on the ground in 90+ countries; ~17K clients including ~80% of Fortune 500; ~$6T payments processed daily to 180+ countries; ~$1T average deposits; $31T AUC/A with 100+ country custody network; ~75% of revenues from Services + Markets + Banking clients. No values are estimated; all figures are printed.

**Design**

Background white #FFFFFF. Title and all body text navy #0F1632. Accent rules and connectors navy #0F1632. Cards light grey #EDEEEF, stat boxes pale blue-grey #C4CDDC, Services box navy #0F1632 with white text #FFFFFF. Typeface CitiSans, a neo-grotesque sans-serif: title in CitiSans Display Bold at about 5.2% of slide height, tagline in CitiSans Display Regular at about 4.4%, card headings in CitiSans Text Regular capitals at about 2.6%, body text regular at about 2.2% to 2.8%, footnote text at about 1.5%. Numerals and symbols such as #1, 11.5%, ~17K, ~$6T, 90+ and 100+ are set in an italic CitiSerif Display face at the same size. Corners of cards and stat boxes are rounded with a radius of about 2% of slide width; no shadows or gradients; lines are about 0.3% of slide height thick.

### Slide 3

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Pairs multi-year bar charts with large market share callouts so the performance story reads at a glance.
- Review: The deck has a clear route: scale and position on slide 2, proof of results on slide 3, resilience on slide 4, then a strategy map on slide 5 that returns as numbered badges on slides 6 to 10 and pays off with the returns trajectory on slide 11.
- Review: Slides 3, 4 and 7 are dense but well organised, with action titles, consistent navy and grey-blue bars that mark the latest year, bracketed change arrows, and a one-line takeaway at the foot of each page.
- Review: The weaknesses are small type on the dense chart panels (slides 3 and 4 read as reference pages), unlabelled bars on slide 7 and the market share bars, and a plain text disclaimer and three pages of tiny footnotes at the end.
- Suggested repair: Raise the smallest type on slides 3, 4 and 7 and add values to the unlabelled bars on slide 7 and slide 11's driver mini-charts so the outcomes can be read without a spoken explanation.
- What would change the score: Split slide 3 or trim its six chart panels so fewer numbers compete, and give slide 2's tree diagram and slide 9's table a stronger focal point.

**Layout**

Title sits top-left across roughly the full width at about 5% from the top, with a bordered KPI strip of seven figures beneath it, a 3-by-2 grid of grey chart panels (three panels in the top row, three in the bottom row) with the EBT panel on the right, a full-width takeaway sentence below the grid, and a small footer line at bottom-left with the page number bottom-right. Left and right margins are about 2.5% of slide width; all panels are left-aligned in their columns.

**Visuals**

Top row, panel 1 (Revenues): vertical bar chart, title REVENUES, subtitle USD in Billions, columns for 2022 to 2025 with values 16.1, 18.6, 20.3 and 22.6 (USD billions); 2022 to 2024 bars light slate blue, 2025 bar navy; an arrow line above the bars labelled +12% CAGR running upward left to right; baseline axis, no y-axis scale shown; category labels 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025 below the baseline. Top row, panel 2 (NIR): vertical bar chart, title NIR, subtitle USD in Billions, columns 2022 to 2025 with values 5.8, 5.4, 6.8 and 7.6 (USD billions); 2022, 2023 and 2024 light slate blue, 2025 navy; arrow labelled +10% CAGR rising left to right; labels 2022, 2023(1), 2024, 2025. Top row, panel 3 (EBT AND RoTCE): combined chart with bars for EBT in USD billions, 2022 7.2, 2023 7.7, 2024 9.4, 2025 11.4 (navy), 1Q26 3.1 (navy); a navy line with dot markers for RoTCE in percent, 2022 17.5%, 2023 16.1%, 2024 22.4%, 2025 24.6%, 1Q26 27.0%; below, a table row labelled Efficiency Ratio with values 54%, 54%, 52%, 48%, 48% under the same year labels 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025, 1Q26. Bottom row, panel 4 (TTS MARKET SHARE Institutional Segment): two bars, 2022 10.0% (light slate blue) and 2025 11.6% (navy); bracket arrow above labelled +~170bps; three ranking lines to the right: #1 Payments(3), #1 Liquidity(3), #1 Trade(3). Bottom row, panel 5 (TTS MARKET SHARE CCB Segment): two bars, 2022 0.5% (light slate blue) and 2025 0.9% (navy); bracket arrow labelled +~35bps; text block to the right: Top 10 TTS CCB(6) and a paragraph about rank and share. Bottom row, panel 6 (SECURITIES SERVICES MARKET SHARE): two bars, 2022 8.8% (light slate blue) and 2025 11.2% (navy); bracket arrow labelled +~240bps; rankings #4 Investor Svcs.(10) and #3 Issuer Svcs.(11) to the right. No highlighted points beyond the navy 2025 bars and the line markers. No legend.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean corporate look on a white page with pale grey #EDEEEF rounded-corner panels. Titles are in deep navy #0F1632 in a bold geometric sans (CitiSans Display Bold, close to a grotesque sans-serif), about 5.2% of slide height for the main title; panel titles are regular-weight caps sans at about 2.6%; body and labels in CitiSans Text Regular at about 2.2%. Numbers in the KPI strip and chart values are set in an italic serif (CitiSerif Display Regular, a Didone-like italic) at about 3.3% for KPI values and 2.2% for chart values. Bars: light slate blue #A3B0CB for earlier years, navy #0F1632 for the latest year. Lines and bracket arrows are navy #0F1632 at about 0.3% of slide height stroke. The KPI strip is outlined by a rounded navy border with thin navy vertical dividers. No shadows or gradients.

### Slide 4

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Places rate-cycle charts beside fee and deposit drivers to show resilience with a clear, single argument.
- Review: The deck has a clear route: scale and position on slide 2, proof of results on slide 3, resilience on slide 4, then a strategy map on slide 5 that returns as numbered badges on slides 6 to 10 and pays off with the returns trajectory on slide 11.
- Review: Slides 3, 4 and 7 are dense but well organised, with action titles, consistent navy and grey-blue bars that mark the latest year, bracketed change arrows, and a one-line takeaway at the foot of each page.
- Review: The weaknesses are small type on the dense chart panels (slides 3 and 4 read as reference pages), unlabelled bars on slide 7 and the market share bars, and a plain text disclaimer and three pages of tiny footnotes at the end.
- Suggested repair: Raise the smallest type on slides 3, 4 and 7 and add values to the unlabelled bars on slide 7 and slide 11's driver mini-charts so the outcomes can be read without a spoken explanation.

**Text**

Services is a through-the-cycle business / Our ability to capture market share and sustain growth through the cycle is driven by consistent / improvement in fees and the stability of our client relationships / GROWTH DRIVERS / SERVICES REVENUE GROWTH ($B) / +~8% CAGR / 22.6 / 20.3 / 18.6 / 16.1 / 13.4 / 12.8 / 12.5 / 13.0 / 12.1 / 11.3 / 6.1 / 2.0 / NIR / 2016 / 2017 / 2018 / 2019 / 2020 / 2021 / 2022 / 2023 / 2024 / 2025 / 1Q26 / Fed Funds / Effective / Rate(1) / 0.40% 1.00% 1.83% 2.16% 0.38% 0.08% 1.68% 5.02% 5.14% 4.21% 3.64% / ↑176bps ↓208bps ↑506bps ↓93bps ↓57bps / NIR 4.7 / EBT 5.5 / AVG. DEPOSITS 530 / AVG. LOANS 72 / Driving sustainable growth… / …through rate cycles / 7.6 6% CAGR / 11.4 8% CAGR / 878 6% CAGR / 93 3% CAGR / ~80% / Services deposits from / 15+ year clients(2) / ~85% / of deposits from clients / using 3+ Services products(2) / DEPOSIT & NII RESILIENCE / Deposit growth and / disciplined beta mgmt. / Balanced mix across NAM / and International(b,3) / FEE DRIVERS (’22-’25 CAGR) / 10% / 6% / 15% / ~3% / Cross-border / Txn Value(f) / USD Clearing / Volume(g) / AUC/A / Global GDP / Growth(4) / IMPROVED SCALABILITY / Fees per Ops / Headcount(e, 5) / +27% / 2022 / 2025 / Deep and longstanding client relationships underpin the resiliency of our business / Footnotes begin on Slide 13 / 4

**Layout**

Title sits top-left at about 2.4% from the left edge, with a two-line subtitle beneath it. A left column (about 2% to 64% of width) holds the revenue bar chart over the rate-change strip and two stacked tables, while a right rounded grey panel (about 67% to 98% of width, 24% to 86% of height) holds the Growth Drivers sections under a small uppercase heading. A centred tagline sits below the content, with a footnote line bottom-left and the page number bottom-right at about 97%.

**Visuals**

Chart: vertical bar chart titled SERVICES REVENUE GROWTH ($B), with a Fed Funds Effective Rate line series and a 2.0 NIR marker. Bars and labelled values in order 2016 11.3, 2017 12.1, 2018 13.0, 2019 13.4, 2020 12.8, 2021 12.5, 2022 16.1, 2023 18.6, 2024 20.3, 2025 22.6, then 1Q26 6.1 (bar drawn after an axis break marked by two slanted strokes). No value axis or units scale is printed beyond the $B title; bars are unlabelled for range. A light blue-grey arrow above the bars runs from about 2016 upward to the right, labelled +~8% CAGR. The Fed Funds Effective Rate line is an unlabelled light blue-grey line (about 0.5 to 5 percent in the row below, values not drawn on the line); its legend sits at left as a short line segment with the text Fed Funds Effective Rate. The 1Q26 bar has a dashed light line inside it at the NIR 2.0 level. Highlight: the 2016 rate value 0.40% and the 2020 to 2021 values 0.38% and 0.08% are set on pale periwinkle rounded rectangles. Rate-change strip: a row of double-headed arrows between the rate values with labels ↑176bps, ↓208bps, ↑506bps, ↓93bps, ↓57bps, bracketed by vertical tick marks. Left table: rows NIR 4.7, EBT 5.5, AVG. DEPOSITS 530, AVG. LOANS 72. Right table: 7.6 at 6% CAGR, 11.4 at 8% CAGR, 878 at 6% CAGR, 93 at 3% CAGR. A navy horizontal arrow with a filled triangular head runs between the two tables, from about 20% to 46% of width at about 76% of height. Growth Drivers panel: contains only text figures and a small bar pair. Bar pair: two vertical bars labelled 2022 (light blue-grey, short) and 2025 (navy, taller, about 18% higher), with a bracket above labelled +27% and a downward arrow to the 2025 bar, all sitting on a thin baseline. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean corporate financial slide with a white background, navy text and graphics, and one pale grey panel. Title, subtitle, tagline and body text are navy #0F1632. Accents are light blue-grey #A3B0CB (CAGR arrow, Fed Funds line, 2022 bar, rule under the rate values) and pale periwinkle #D6DDF5 (rate highlight pills). Typefaces: CitiSans Display Bold for the title, CitiSans Text Regular for subtitle and labels, CitiSans Display Regular for the tagline and arrow captions, CitiSans Text Bold for the year labels and rate-row label, and CitiSerif Display Regular (italic-style figures) for all numerals and percentages. Title is about 5.2% of slide height, subtitle and tagline about 3.7%, section headings and labels about 2.2% to 2.6%, body and captions about 1.7% to 2.0%, footnote about 1.5%. Bars are solid navy rectangles with square corners and no shadow. The grey panel has rounded corners (about 1.2% of width), no border and no shadow, with thin dotted grey dividers between its sections. Rules are thin grey horizontal lines.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

### Slide 5

**Review notes**

- Review: The deck has a clear route: scale and position on slide 2, proof of results on slide 3, resilience on slide 4, then a strategy map on slide 5 that returns as numbered badges on slides 6 to 10 and pays off with the returns trajectory on slide 11.
- Worth keeping: Keep the numbered strategy thread from slide 5 through slides 6 to 10, the navy highlight for the current year, the change arrows with CAGR callouts, and the closing takeaway line on each page.

**Text**

Industry trends and our strategy / The industry faces disruption from innovative technology, creating a mix of risks and opportunities / INDUSTRY TRENDS / KEY FOCUS AREAS / 1 / Execute on client strategy / New technologies: AI and blockchain / Continued growth in digital commerce and / cross-border solutions / 2 / Next-generation platforms / Supply chain reconfiguration / AI initiatives / Ongoing expansion of ETF and private markets AUM / 3 / Innovative solutions / Digital Asset strategy / Demand for seamless client experience / Our clients require integrated 24/7, real-time solutions to navigate this landscape / 5

**Layout**

Two-column layout below a full-width title block: the title sits top-left at x 2.6% y 5.5% with a one-line subtitle beneath it at y 12.4%. The left column (INDUSTRY TRENDS, x 2.7% to 47.7%) holds five stacked light grey rows, and the right column (KEY FOCUS AREAS, x 54.5% to 97.4%) holds three navy numbered bars, each followed by a light blue sub-bar for bars 2 and 3. A vertical dark brace separates the columns at x about 49.5% to 52%. A full-width banner statement with left and right arrows sits at y 89% to 93%, and the slide number is bottom right at x 97%, y 97.5%. All text is left aligned except the focus-area labels, which are centred; left and right margins are about 2.5%.

**Visuals**

Non-text elements: (1) A vertical dark navy brace line, drawn as a straight vertical bar at x about 49.5% spanning y 26% to 82.5%, with a right-pointing chevron in the middle (about y 50% to 58%, tip at x about 52%). Stroke colour #0F1632, stroke width about 0.3% of slide width, square joins. (2) Five light grey rounded rectangles (#EDEEEF) in the left column holding trend text. (3) Three dark navy rounded rectangles (#0F1632) in the right column, each with an italic serif numeral at left, numbered 1, 2 and 3. (4) Two light blue-grey rounded rectangles (#C4CDDC) under bars 2 and 3, labelled AI initiatives and Digital Asset strategy. (5) Two elbow connector lines, dark navy, about 0.3% stroke, running down from the bottom edge of bar 2 and bar 3 at x about 56.6% and turning right into the sub-bars at x about 59.6% with small filled triangle arrowheads. (6) A banner with a dark navy horizontal arrow pointing left at x 2.4% to 9% and a dark navy arrow pointing right at x 91% to 97.6%, both at y about 91%, with a white rectangle masking the arrow shaft behind the statement text. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF, no gradient, photograph or pattern. Clean corporate slide with a flat white background and no gradients, shadows or textures. Titles and body text are navy #0F1632. Dark accent boxes are navy #0F1632 with white text. Left trend rows are light grey #EDEEEF. Sub-bars are light blue-grey #C4CDDC. Typefaces: CitiSans Display Bold for the title and focus-area headings, CitiSans Text Regular for the subtitle, labels and trend rows, CitiSans Display Regular for sub-bar labels and the banner, and a serif display italic (CitiSerif Display Regular) for numerals and the 24/7 mark. Title text is about 5.2% of slide height, subtitle and banner about 3.3% to 3.7%, body and trend rows about 2.6%, labels about 3.0%. Corner radius on boxes is small, about 1% of slide width. Thin outlines are absent; only the brace and connectors are stroked.

**Layout**

Three-column grid under a title row: a navy numbered circle badge sits at top left with the title 'Execute on client strategy' to its right, left-aligned at the top. Each column has a centred two-line uppercase heading, a navy downward chevron, three grey rounded chips, a bullet list, and a row of two small charts sitting on a shared baseline near the bottom; thin vertical navy divider rules separate the columns. The footer note sits bottom left and the page number bottom right, with margins of about 2.5% on the left and right.

**Visuals**

Column 1 'DEEPEN WITH INSTITUTIONAL CLIENTS' has two charts. (a) Pie chart 'SERVICES CLUSTER MIX (Revenues)' with footnote marker (3): seven slices labelled NAM (pale blue-grey, about 15%), LATAM (#A3B0CB, about 12%), Europe (mid grey, about 14%), UK (black, about 8%), MEA (white with thin outline, about 14%), JANA (light grey, about 12%), Asia South (dark navy, about 12%); no values printed, so shares are estimates marked about; no legend, labels sit outside the slices. (b) Pie chart 'SERVICES CLIENT MIX (Revenues)' with footnote marker (4): two slices, Corporate (navy #0F1632, about 60%) and FI (#A3B0CB, about 40%); no values printed. Column 2 'SUPERCHARGE STRATEGIC CLIENT SEGMENTS' has two bar charts. (c) 'SERVICES CCB REVENUE GROWTH(h)': two navy-and-blue-grey columns, 2022 in #A3B0CB at about 40% of plot height and 2025 in #0F1632 at about 60%, with a dark navy trend arrow rising from left to right above them and the label '+12% CAGR'; baseline axis rule under the bars; no axis values printed. (d) 'ASSET MANAGER & OWNER AUC/A': 2022 bar #A3B0CB at about 35% height, 2025 bar #0F1632 at about 65%, trend arrow with label '+14% CAGR'. Column 3 'CAPTURE SHARE IN HIGH-GROWTH VERTICALS' has two charts. (e) 'AVERAGE DAILY INSTANT PAYMENT TRANSACTIONS(5)': 2022 bar #A3B0CB at about 50% of 2025 height, 2025 bar #0F1632; a thin navy bracket with arrowhead pointing down from 2022 to 2025 labelled '~250%'. (f) 'NAM ETF AUC/A GROWTH(6)': 2022 bar #A3B0CB at about 15% height, 2025 bar #0F1632 at about 60%; thin navy bracket with arrowhead labelled '250%+'. No logos, photographs or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Clean white corporate slide with navy #0F1632 for title, headings, body, chart labels and dark bars. Accent colours are pale blue-grey #A3B0CB and light grey chips #EDEEEF. Title is a bold geometric sans (CitiSans Display Bold, sans-serif) at about 5.2% of slide height; the number '1' is an italic serif (CitiSerif Display) in white inside the navy circle. Column headings are regular uppercase sans (CitiSans Text Regular) at about 3.0% height. Body bullets are regular sans at about 2.4% height; numerals and percentages in charts are italic serif display. Chart years are bold sans at about 2.2%. Chips are rounded rectangles with small corner radius (about 8% of chip height), no shadow, no border. Chevrons are solid navy triangles. Divider lines are thin navy hairlines about 0.1% width. No gradients or shadows.

### Slide 7

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Uses large metric tiles with before-and-after figures to make platform outcomes easy to compare.
- Review: The deck has a clear route: scale and position on slide 2, proof of results on slide 3, resilience on slide 4, then a strategy map on slide 5 that returns as numbered badges on slides 6 to 10 and pays off with the returns trajectory on slide 11.
- Review: Slides 3, 4 and 7 are dense but well organised, with action titles, consistent navy and grey-blue bars that mark the latest year, bracketed change arrows, and a one-line takeaway at the foot of each page.
- Review: The weaknesses are small type on the dense chart panels (slides 3 and 4 read as reference pages), unlabelled bars on slide 7 and the market share bars, and a plain text disclaimer and three pages of tiny footnotes at the end.
- Worth keeping: Keep the numbered strategy thread from slide 5 through slides 6 to 10, the navy highlight for the current year, the change arrows with CAGR callouts, and the closing takeaway line on each page.
- Suggested repair: Raise the smallest type on slides 3, 4 and 7 and add values to the unlabelled bars on slide 7 and slide 11's driver mini-charts so the outcomes can be read without a spoken explanation.

**Text**

2 / Next-generation platforms / Building next-generation platforms to support the future of transaction services with speed, scale and availability / PLATFORM OBJECTIVES / INVESTMENT OUTCOMES / 1 / Real-Time, Scalable, Always-On / • High volume acceptance / • Low latency processing: 99.9%+ availability(1) / • 10B+ API calls ('22-'25)(2) / 2 / Architecture and Engineering Excellence / • Simplified and automated estate / • Cyber protection / • #1 (tied) TTS Online Portal for Global Capabilities(3) / 3 / Strong Data and AI Foundation / • Global data standards / • Proactive risk management / • Actionable client insights / APPLICATIONS ON MODERN ARCHITECTURE(4) / 2x / 2022 / 2025 / TRANSACTIONS PER DAY (Citi® Payments Express - live in 22 markets(a, b)) / 0 / ~10MM(5) / 2022 / 2025(5) / CUSTODY SETTLEMENT VOLUME AND UNIT COST(6) / +37% / Unit cost -25% / 2022 / 2025 / CLIENT SATISFACTION / TTS(7) / +5pp / Investor Services(8) / +5pp / 2022 2025 / 2023 2025 / We continue to invest over $2B in our technology and data platforms, innovative solutions and client experience / Footnotes begin on Slide 13 / 7

**Layout**

Two-column body under a full-width header: the numbered title sits top-left with the subtitle beneath it, and a footer line sits bottom-left with the slide number bottom-right. The left column holds three stacked rounded panels titled PLATFORM OBJECTIVES, and the right column holds a 2x2 grid of small bar charts titled INVESTMENT OUTCOMES, separated by a thin vertical rule at about x 50. A centred closing statement sits below both columns, and all text is left-aligned except the closing statement, which is centred.

**Visuals**

Four small bar-chart panels in the right column, all in navy #0F1632 and light blue-grey #A3B1CB on a white field, with no gridlines and a horizontal baseline under each. (1) Applications on modern architecture: a 2022 bar about half the height of a 2025 bar; a bracket arrow above labelled 2x runs from the 2022 bar top to the 2025 bar top; x-axis labels 2022 and 2025; no value axis. (2) Transactions per day (Citi Payments Express, live in 22 markets): 2022 value shown as 0 with no bar; 2025 bar labelled ~10MM(5); a thin diagonal arrow rises from above the 2022 baseline to the upper right; x-axis labels 2022 and 2025(5). (3) Custody settlement volume and unit cost: 2022 bar in light blue-grey and 2025 bar in navy, the 2025 bar about 1.15 times the 2022 bar height (about); bracket arrow labelled +37%; a grey line segment crosses both bars sloping downward to the right; a side label reads Unit cost -25%; x-axis labels 2022 and 2025. (4) Client satisfaction: two mini charts, TTS(7) with 2022 light bar and 2025 navy bar, and Investor Services(8) with 2023 light bar and 2025 navy bar; each has a bracket arrow labelled +5pp; x-axis labels 2022 2025 and 2023 2025. No numeric value axes or legends. The three objective panels contain no graphics other than thin white vertical dividers.

**Design**

Background: Plain white #FFFFFF.

**Review notes**

- Review: The deck has a clear route: scale and position on slide 2, proof of results on slide 3, resilience on slide 4, then a strategy map on slide 5 that returns as numbered badges on slides 6 to 10 and pays off with the returns trajectory on slide 11.
- Worth keeping: Keep the numbered strategy thread from slide 5 through slides 6 to 10, the navy highlight for the current year, the change arrows with CAGR callouts, and the closing takeaway line on each page.

**Layout**

Full-width white slide with a left margin of about 2.2% and a right margin of about 2.2%. A navy numbered badge and the title sit top left, with the subtitle beneath the title, left-aligned at about 9.2%. Below, two columns: five column cards on the left (2.2% to 51.2%) and a numbered priority list on the right (54.9% to 95%), split by a vertical hairline at 51.2%. A row of four outlined stat boxes spans the bottom, with a footnote line bottom-left and the slide number bottom-right.

**Visuals**

Five vertical rounded rectangles in graded fills, left to right: SALES ASSIST #D5DDE0, CLIENT ASSIST #C5CEDD, OPS ASSIST #A2B1CC, AGENT ASSIST #3D4560, TECH ASSIST #0F1632, each 8.7% wide and 40% tall, top at 30.6%, with white-to-navy text. A thin vertical light-grey hairline at x 51.2% runs from 30.6% to 71.3%. A solid navy circle at top left holds an italic serif numeral 2 in white. A numbered list of four priorities with italic serif numerals 1 to 4 and light grey horizontal dividers at about 40%, 50% and 60% height. Four white rounded outline boxes with navy strokes along the bottom, each with a title, a large italic serif figure and a description: DEVELOPER PRODUCTIVITY ~30-40%; INTELLIGENT DOCUMENT PROCESSING 80%; AGENT ASSIST ~20-25%; CitiDirect AI ASSISTANT ~50%. The box titles break the top stroke where they sit, masked by white fills. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF.

### Slide 9

**Review notes**

- Review: The deck has a clear route: scale and position on slide 2, proof of results on slide 3, resilience on slide 4, then a strategy map on slide 5 that returns as numbered badges on slides 6 to 10 and pays off with the returns trajectory on slide 11.
- Worth keeping: Keep the numbered strategy thread from slide 5 through slides 6 to 10, the navy highlight for the current year, the change arrows with CAGR callouts, and the closing takeaway line on each page.
- What would change the score: Split slide 3 or trim its six chart panels so fewer numbers compete, and give slide 2's tree diagram and slide 9's table a stronger focal point.

**Text**

3 / Innovative solutions / Our investments are focused on serving our clients’ evolving needs with best-in-class client experience / PAYMENTS / • 24/7 Clearing – now integrated with Citi® Token Services / • Cross-border and WorldLink® payments / 300+ / Banks live with 24/7 Clearing(b,1) / 135+ / Cross currency payment network(b) / LIQUIDITY / • Real-time liquidity management solutions / • Liquidity structures – facilitating $24T+ in sweeps annually / 80+ / Country liquidity pooling network(a,b) / ~50% / Of F500 clients using Citi liquidity structures(b,2) / TRADE / • Leading supply chain finance platform - ~440k buyer/supplier relationships(3) / • Global platform enables digital delivery connected to Investor/Funder / ~$1T / Throughput(4) / 27K / Buyer/Supplier relationships added YoY(5) / INVESTOR SERVICES / • Single event processing / • ETF platform / 95% reduction / In U.S. elective corporate action event notification time driven by SEP(6) / ~50% / NAM ETF AUC/A CAGR (’22-’25) / ISSUER SERVICES / • Digital issuance capabilities / • Leading provider of depositary receipts services / $1B / Digitally Native Note issuances(b) / 14 / Global Depositary Notes markets live(a,b) / Footnotes begin on Slide 13 / 9

**Layout**

Section badge and title sit top left, with the title beside the badge; a two-line subtitle starts under the title at the same left edge. Below, five full-width rows are stacked with equal spacing: each row has a grey rounded label panel on the left (category name and bullets) and two metric columns on the right, with thin light grey dividers between rows. Footnote at bottom left and page number at bottom right, both on the same baseline near the bottom margin.

**Visuals**

No photographs, charts or logos. Visual elements are a dark navy filled circle behind the section numeral 3, and five light grey rounded rectangles (one per row) holding the category labels and bullets, plus four thin horizontal divider lines between the rows. Metric figures are set in large italic serif numerals with small sans-serif captions beneath, and no chart or table is drawn.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF. Plain white background with navy text. Titles and the section badge are navy #0F1632 and metric numerals are near-black #161616. Title is a bold geometric sans (CitiSans Display Bold, about 5.2% of slide height); subtitle and bullets are regular sans (CitiSans Text Regular, about 3.7% and 2.4%). Metric numerals use a light display serif (CitiSerif Display Regular, about 3.0%) with captions in regular sans (about 2.2%). Row panels are flat #EDEEEF with corner radius of roughly 1.5% of slide width, no shadows or gradients. Dividers are thin light grey lines.

### Slide 11

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Sets a returns trajectory against key drivers in one view, tying targets to specific initiatives.
- Review: The deck has a clear route: scale and position on slide 2, proof of results on slide 3, resilience on slide 4, then a strategy map on slide 5 that returns as numbered badges on slides 6 to 10 and pays off with the returns trajectory on slide 11.
- Suggested repair: Raise the smallest type on slides 3, 4 and 7 and add values to the unlabelled bars on slide 7 and slide 11's driver mini-charts so the outcomes can be read without a spoken explanation.

**Text**

Preeminent partner for institutions with cross-border needs / Execute on / client strategy / Invest in / next-generation / platforms / Embed AI across / the client lifecycle / Continue to / develop innovative / solutions / Build on / foundational digital / asset investments / KEY DRIVERS / RETURNS TRAJECTORY / AUC/A ($T) / +15% CAGR / Mid-single- / digit CAGR / 21 / 31 / 2022 / 2025 / Near term(j) / AVERAGE DEPOSITS ($B) / +3% CAGR / Mid-single- / digit CAGR / 809 / 878 / 2022 / 2025 / Near term(j) / CROSS BORDER TXN VALUE(f) ($B) / +10% CAGR / Mid-single- / digit CAGR / 312 / 416 / 2022 / 2025 / Near term(j) / 17.5% / 24.6% / Mid-20s / 2022(d) / 2025(d) / Through the Cycle(i) / Targeting low to mid-single-digit revenue CAGR in the near term / and mid-20’s RoTCE through the cycle / Footnotes begin on Slide 13 / 11

**Layout**

Full-width title sits top-left at about 5% of slide height, left-aligned at a 2.5% margin, in a single line. Below it a row of five equal light-grey rounded boxes spans the width at y 12% to 28%, each 17.4% wide with 2% gaps, joined by a thin navy connector line. Beneath, the left half holds three small bar charts in a 2x2-style arrangement (AUC/A and Average Deposits on top, separated by a vertical dashed divider; Cross Border Txn Value bottom-centre of the left half, separated by a horizontal dashed rule), and the right half holds the Returns Trajectory column chart with a chevron bracket at its left. A centred two-line tagline sits at y 84% to 92%, and a footer at y 97.5% bottom-left with the slide number bottom-right, both at about 1.5% of slide height.

**Visuals**

Five-box process row: five light grey (#EDEEEF) rounded rectangles, each labelled, left to right: Execute on client strategy; Invest in next-generation platforms; Embed AI across the client lifecycle; Continue to develop innovative solutions; Build on foundational digital asset investments. A navy (#0F1632) horizontal line runs behind them, visible as short dark connectors between boxes. Chart 1 (KEY DRIVERS, top left) is a column chart titled AUC/A ($T); columns for 2022 = 21 and 2025 = 31 (navy #0F1632) and Near term shown as a light blue-grey (#A3B0CB) column with no printed value (about +15% CAGR arrow from 2022 to 2025 printed above, and label Mid-single-digit CAGR above the Near term column). The 2022 column is about 21 units high and the 2025 column about 31 units high, scaled to a common baseline; no axis values or ranges are printed. Chart 2 is a column chart titled AVERAGE DEPOSITS ($B): 2022 = 809 and 2025 = 878 in navy columns, Near term light blue-grey column with no value, +3% CAGR arrow, Mid-single-digit CAGR label. No axes or legend printed. Chart 3 is a column chart titled CROSS BORDER TXN VALUE(f) ($B): 2022 = 312 and 2025 = 416 navy columns, Near term light blue-grey column with no value, +10% CAGR arrow, Mid-single-digit CAGR label. Chart 4 (RETURNS TRAJECTORY, right) is a column chart with no axes or gridlines: 2022(d) column navy labelled 17.5%, about 9.6% of slide height tall; 2025(d) navy column labelled 24.6%, about 22% tall; Through the Cycle(i) light blue-grey column labelled Mid-20s (no numeric value), about 22.7% tall, all sitting on a thin navy baseline. Each sparkline arrow is a thin navy line with arrowhead rising slightly left to right. A thin navy chevron-shaped bracket (open to the left, angle pointing right) sits between the left charts and the returns chart, about 27% of slide height tall. Dashed grey vertical divider and horizontal dashed rule separate the three small charts. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid white #FFFFFF, full slide. Clean corporate style on white. Titles and headings navy #0E1632; body and chart labels navy #0F1632; the lighter bars and Near term/Mid-20s columns are light blue-grey #A3B0CB; box fills #EDEEEF light grey; dark bars and connector navy #0F1632. Typefaces: CitiSans (a humanist sans-serif) in Display Bold for the title, Text Regular for box labels and section headings in all capitals, Text Bold for chart year labels, and CitiSerif Display (a serif, shown in italic) for numeric values and CAGR percentages. Title is about 5.2% of slide height (large, bold); box text and chart labels range about 2.0% to 2.6%; section headers about 3.0%; tagline about 3.7%; footer about 1.5%. Boxes have rounded corners of about 1.2% of width, no border, no shadow. No gradients.

### Slide 13

**Text**

Footnotes
Global
a. Represents both countries and jurisdictions.
b. As of December 31, 2025.
c. As previously disclosed, certain reporting changes were implemented in the first quarter of 2026. Prior period results for Citi's segments were recast to reflect these reporting changes, while Citi's consolidated results remained unchanged. For
additional information, please refer to Citi's Form 8-K furnished with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission on April 3, 2026.
d. Return on Tangible Common Equity (RoTCE) is a non-GAAP financial measure. RoTCE represents annualized net income available to common shareholders as a percentage of average TCE. For a reconciliation to reported results, please refer to
Citi’s Form 8-K furnished with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission on April 3, 2026 and the Citigroup Inc. Quarterly Financial Data Supplement for the quarter ended March 31, 2026, which is Exhibit 99.2 to Citigroup's Current Report
on Form 8-K furnished with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission on April 14, 2026.
e. Fee income is earned for assisting clients with transactional services and clearing. Revenue generated from these activities is recorded in Commissions and fees. Revenue is also generated from assets under custody and administration (AUC/A)
and is primarily recorded in Administration and other fiduciary fees.
f. Cross-border Transaction Value is defined as the total value of Cross-border FX Payments processed through Citi’s proprietary WorldLink and Cross-border Funds Transfer platforms, including payments from Consumer, Corporate, Financial
Institution and Public Sector clients.
g. U.S. Dollar Clearing Volume is defined as the number of USD Clearing Payment instructions processed by Citi on behalf of U.S. and foreign-domiciled entities (primarily Financial Institutions).
h. Represents portion of the Commercial Bank (CCB) revenue aligned with Services. CCB is defined as a sales coverage unit for Citi clients and is not an operating segment or reporting unit. The financial results for CCB are reflected across various
products within the Services, Markets, and Banking segments. The CCB client segmentation is principally determined by several factors, including product needs, client organizational complexity, origin of the client relationship, as well as size
(with CCB generally targeting companies with up to $3 billion in annual revenues). To ensure effective client service, these client segmentation criteria are periodically reviewed and updated, and the information presented reflects current criteria
across all comparative periods.
i. RoTCE through the cycle is a forward-looking non-GAAP financial measure. From time-to-time, management may discuss forward-looking non-GAAP financial measures, such as forward-looking estimates or targets for revenue, expenses, and
RoTCE. We are unable to provide a reconciliation of forward-looking non-GAAP financial measures to their most directly comparable GAAP financial measures because we are unable to provide, without unreasonable effort, a meaningful or
accurate calculation or estimation of amounts that would be necessary for the reconciliation due to the complexity and inherent difficulty in forecasting and quantifying future amounts or when they may occur. Such unavailable information could
be significant for future results. All through-the-cycle returns amounts are financial targets and subject to macro and market conditions.
j. Citi defines the near-term period as 2027-2028. Citi expects to be within the near-term RoTCE range for both 2027 and 2028. Near-term CAGR targets refer to the period from 2025-2028. Citi expects to reach near-term non-CAGR targets over
the near term. All near-term amounts are targets and subject to macro and market conditions.

U.S. Dollar Clearing Volume is defined as the number of USD Clearing Payment instructions processed by Citi on behalf of U.S. and foreign-domiciled entities (primarily Financial Institutions). / h. Represents portion of the Commercial Bank (CCB) revenue aligned with Services. CCB is defined as a sales coverage unit for Citi clients and is not an operating segment or reporting unit. The financial results for CCB are reflected across various / products within the Services, Markets, and Banking segments. The CCB client segmentation is principally determined by several factors, including product needs, client organizational complexity, origin of the client relationship, as well as size / (with CCB generally targeting companies with up to $3 billion in annual revenues). To ensure effective client service, these client segmentation criteria are periodically reviewed and updated, and the information presented reflects current criteria / across all comparative periods. / i. RoTCE through the cycle is a forward-looking non-GAAP financial measure. From time-to-time, management may discuss forward-looking non-GAAP financial measures, such as forward-looking estimates or targets for revenue, expenses, and / RoTCE. We are unable to provide a reconciliation of forward-looking non-GAAP financial measures to their most directly comparable GAAP financial measures because we are unable to provide, without unreasonable effort, a meaningful or / accurate calculation or estimation of amounts that would be necessary for the reconciliation due to the complexity and inherent difficulty in forecasting and quantifying future amounts or when they may occur. Such unavailable information could / be significant for future results. All through-the-cycle returns amounts are financial targets and subject to macro and market conditions. / j. Citi defines the near-term period as 2027-2028. Citi expects to be within the near-term RoTCE range for both 2027 and 2028. Near-term CAGR targets refer to the period from 2025-2028. Citi expects to reach near-term non-CAGR targets over / the near term. All near-term amounts are targets and subject to macro and market conditions. ### Slide 15

**Text**

Footnotes
Slide 7
1. Represents Citi® Payments Express Instant Payment availability in December 2025, excluding Argentina.
2. API call is a request made by one software application to another through an Application Programming Interface (API).
3. Source: Crisil Coalition Greenwich 2025 Digital Transformation Benchmarking Program
4. Represents the increase in the number of production applications on modernized tech stack from 2022-2025. Modern architecture is defined as any application based on micro services that can be deployed on containers.
5. Data represents December 2025, where up to 10MM transactions were processed per day by Citi® Payments Express.
6. Custody unit cost represents the operational expense incurred to process and settle a single trade. It is determined by dividing the total US dollar cost of the operational staff dedicated to trade processing within a calendar month by the total number of trades processed and settled during that same month.
7. Source: Source: TTS Voice of the Client (VoC) annual survey programs in 2022 and 2025. Client satisfaction represents the sum of the percentages of respondents who rated “very satisfied” and “satisfied” on overall satisfaction with Citi TTS relationship. 2022 to 2025 growth is shown in percentage points.
8. Source: Investor Services Voice of the Client (VoC) Survey is administered by NMG Consulting on an annual basis. Client satisfaction represents the Overall Satisfaction with Citi by firms who responded with a Very Satisfied (5) or Satisfied (4) rating based on a scale of 1 to 5. 2023 to 2025 growth is shown in percentage points.
Slide 8
1. Target developer productivity is measured as the reduction in time it takes a developer to deliver a work effort with AI tools compared to their estimated delivery time.
2. Represents the reduction in deal document review processing time for U.S. resident account activities, since Intelligent Document Processing functionality was released in December 2025.
3. Represents the projected reduction in client servicing work effort time through the use of Agent Assist AI capabilities. Agent Assist was launched in March 2026.
4. In 2026 in the United States.
Slide 9
1. Source is Citi Payment Processor (CPP).
2. Liquidity structures include notional pooling, physical pooling, Citi Interest Optimization and Virtual Accounts.
3. Represents number of Buyer/Supplier relationships as of March 2026. Buyer/Supplier relationships are defined as unique combinations of buyer entities and seller entities in Citi Supplier Finance.
4. Reflects the total amount of FY25 trade flows through Citi’s systems.
5. Represents increase in Buyer/Supplier relationships from April 2025 to March 2026.
6. Represents the reduction in U.S. elective corporate action event notification time (measured in hours) post-migration to Single Event Processing (SEP) on Feb 28, 2026.
Slide 10
1. Tokenized money market fund. This is a prospective solution under evaluation.
2. Virtual Asset Service Provider.
